<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> Fix half the labels in a Thorp shuffle on $`2^d`$ positions and reveal their complete trajectories. We prove that, after an explicit absolute number of coordinate sweeps, the conditional permutation of the remaining labels approaches uniform in expected total variation as $`d\to\infty`$, uniformly in the initial layout. The resulting constructions give full-deck mixing in a constant multiple of <i>d</i> physical shuffles.

- **claim-conditional-permutation-mixing** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-methods)): Let $n=2^d$, and start the shuffle from any deterministic deck. After $J(s_0+1)$ sweeps, where $s_0=400$ and $J=40000$, the conditional free-slot law defined above satisfies $$\sup_{\text{initial layouts}}\mathbb E_{\mathcal E} \\|\nu_{\mathcal E}-U_{n/2}\\|_{\mathrm{TV}}\longrightarrow0 \qquad(d\longrightarrow\infty).$$ Consequently the full-deck law approaches uniform, uniformly in the starting deck, after $16\,040\,400d$ physical shuffles. For every integer $t\ge0$, after $t$ physical shuffles the full-deck distance from uniform is at least $1-2^{tn/2}/n!$. Thus the full-deck mixing time is of order $d$.
  - Explanation: After a fixed number of sweeps, the permutation of the free slots, conditional on the revealed outside paths, is close to uniform on average, uniformly in the starting layout. This yields full-deck mixing in order d physical shuffles, with a matching support-count lower bound.
- **claim-slot-bijection-kernel** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-slot-kernel)): Fix a deterministic starting layout and a set of outside labels. For any feasible specification $e$ of their paths through a finite interval, conditional on $e$ all switch values on edges incident to an outside card are fixed, and all other switch values are independent fair bits. Let $F_s$ be the set of free positions at boundary $s$, and let $m=\|F_s\|$. Write $\iota_s:[m]\to F_s$ for the increasing bijection. Every completion of the unexposed bits induces a bijection $T_e:F_0\to F_T$, hence a permutation $$g_e=\iota_T^{-1}T_e\iota_0\in S_m.$$ Its conditional law depends on the outside paths and positions, but not on the incoming assignment of free labels. On consecutive intervals the corresponding permutations multiply with later intervals on the left. Conditional on all the outside paths, their remaining switch arrays are independent, so their conditional laws compose by convolution.
  - Explanation: Once the outside paths are fixed, the remaining switches stay independent fair bits. They induce a random bijection between free slots, which becomes a permutation of one fixed group. These permutations compose by convolution across consecutive intervals.
- **claim-coarse-block-estimates** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-coarse-block-estimates)): For the one-sweep matrix $Q$ defined above and every free-slot row vector $u$, a good starting blocked set $E$ satisfies $$\mathbb E\\|uQ\\|^2=u A_E u^\top \le \\|u_{\rm c}\\|^2+q^{d-h}\\|u\\|^2. \label{special:burn-active:eq:2}$$ For every starting blocked set $E$ with $\|E\|\le n/2$, the ending set satisfies $$\mathbb P(E'\text{ not good})\le \epsilon_n. \label{special:burn-active:eq:3}$$ If $u$ has sum zero, then its coarse part on the ending free slots satisfies $$\mathbb E\\|(uQ)_{\rm c}\\|^2\le (4\cdot 2^{-h}+\epsilon_n)\\|u\\|^2. \label{special:burn-active:eq:4}$$
  - Explanation: A single sweep contracts the fluctuation of a free-slot vector inside coarse blocks. The expected energy of the output block averages is small. An unbalanced blocked output set has tiny probability.
- **claim-half-deck-preparation** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-half-preparation)): For all sufficiently large $d$, every ordered list of $k\le n/2$ distinct labels, from every deterministic starting layout, has endpoint law within $n^{-3}$ in total variation of uniform ordered distinct positions after $400$ sweeps.
  - Explanation: After 400 sweeps the endpoint law of any ordered list of at most half the labels is within a polynomially small total variation distance of uniform ordered distinct positions.
- **claim-character-fixed-points** (bound, [source](#stmt-input-character)): Fix $0<\delta<1/4$. For all sufficiently large integers $r$, every irreducible character $\chi$ of $S_r$, of degree $D=\chi(1)$, and every $\sigma\in S_r$ with $f$ fixed points satisfy $$\begin{aligned} f\le r^{1-4\delta}&\quad\Longrightarrow\quad \frac{\|\chi(\sigma)\|}{D}\le D^{-\delta},\label{eq:character-small-fixed}\\ \frac{\|\chi(\sigma)\|}{D}&\le D^{-\delta}r^{\delta f}.\label{eq:character-all-fixed} \end{aligned}$$ The threshold for $r$ depends only on $\delta$.
  - Explanation: The normalized character of a symmetric-group irreducible at a permutation is small in a negative power of the degree. The bound is uniform and depends on the number of fixed points, in a form that holds also for many fixed points.
- **claim-reciprocal-degree-sum** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-degree-sum)): For the irreducible degrees $D_\lambda$ of $S_r$, $$\sum_{\lambda:\,D_\lambda>1}D_\lambda^{-2}=o(1) \qquad(r\longrightarrow\infty).\label{eq:degree-square-sum}$$
  - Explanation: The sum of reciprocal squares of the nontrivial irreducible degrees of the symmetric group tends to zero as the size grows. It controls every Fourier sum used in the constructions.
- **claim-reset-full-deck-mixing** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-reset)): For $n=2^d$, the full-deck law from any deterministic start has total-variation distance tending to zero from uniform after $171798691968d$ physical shuffles.
  - Explanation: The reset-minorization construction gives full-deck mixing from any deterministic start after an explicit constant multiple of d physical shuffles.
- **claim-reset-mass-contraction** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-reset-mass-contraction)): Fix $0<\rho\le1$. For all sufficiently large $d$ depending only on $\rho$, any deterministic hole placement with $h\ge\rho N$ and any initial signed masses supported on the holes and of total mass zero have expected squared norm after two sweeps at most $$N^{-\rho/16}L,$$ where $L$ is their initial squared norm. The masses evolve by the conditional averaging and transport rules just described. The estimate also holds conditionally on the past when the two sweeps begin.
  - Explanation: Signed masses on holes with total mass zero lose a polynomial factor of squared norm after two sweeps, in expectation, whenever the holes keep a fixed positive density. The bound holds conditionally on the past.
- **claim-reset-partial-mixing** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-reset-partial-mixing)): For fixed $0<\rho\le1$, set $s=\lceil320/\rho\rceil$. For all sufficiently large $d$ depending only on $\rho$, after $2s$ sweeps the positions of any ordered tuple of $b\le(1-\rho)N$ distinct labels are within $\varepsilon_N=N^{-8}$ in total variation of the uniform ordered injection, from any deterministic start. The same assertion holds for sweeps in reverse order $d,\ldots,1$.
  - Explanation: After a number of sweeps determined by the omitted fraction, any ordered tuple of labels with a fixed positive fraction omitted is polynomially close to the uniform ordered injection. The same holds in the reversed coordinate order.
- **claim-reset-minorization** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-reset-minorization)): Let $K$ be the kernel of the $2s$ forward sweeps in Proposition <a href="#prop:reset-partial-mixing" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:reset-partial-mixing">9</a> on an ordered injection state space $\Omega$ with $b\le (1-\rho)N$ labels. Then $$K^2(x,y)\ge \frac{p_0}{\|\Omega\|},\qquad p_0=1-2\varepsilon_N.$$
  - Explanation: Combining the forward and reverse partial-mixing bounds gives a pointwise uniform lower bound on the two-step kernel of the forward reset sweeps. This is the minorization used to split off an exact uniform component.
- **claim-reset-conditional-uniformity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-reset-conditional)): For the run just defined, with $\rho=2^{-20}$, $s=\lceil320/\rho\rceil$ and $l=128$ blocks of $4s$ reset sweeps followed by one mixing sweep, $$\mathbb E\\|p_{\mathcal E}-u_{\mathcal E}\\|_{\rm TV}\longrightarrow0 \qquad(d\longrightarrow\infty),$$ uniformly over deterministic starting decks. The environment $\mathcal E$ reveals every reset coin and the nonhole paths during the mixing sweeps, as above.
  - Explanation: After the reset blocks, the assignment of the hole labels is close to uniform in expected total variation, even when every reset coin and the nonhole paths during the mixing sweeps are revealed.
- **claim-overlay-full-deck-mixing** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-overlay)): For $n=2^d$, the full-deck law from any deterministic start has total-variation distance tending to zero from uniform after $33030144d$ physical shuffles.
  - Explanation: The trajectory-overlay construction gives full-deck mixing from any deterministic start after an explicit constant multiple of d physical shuffles.
- **claim-overlay-preparation** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-overlay-preparation)): After $20d$ layers, the ordered positions of any $q$ specified cards in the base shuffle have total-variation distance $o(1)$ from uniform ordered distinct positions. The estimate is uniform in their starting positions and in the starting cyclic phase.
  - Explanation: After a preparation of order d layers, the ordered positions of a fixed fraction of specified cards in the base shuffle are close to uniform ordered distinct positions, uniformly in the starting positions and cyclic phase.
- **claim-overlay-group-call** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-overlay-call)): Let $A\subset V$ have $q=n/8$ indices. Use $k=65536$ phases, each consisting of $20d$ preparation layers without extra switches and one active sweep with extra switches on the pairs whose two base indices belong to $A$. Let $\kappa_{\mathcal B}$ be the law of the resulting multiplier on $\mathfrak S(A)$ conditional on the full base shuffle $\mathcal B$. Then $$\sup_{\text{pre-call base histories}} \mathbb E\big[\\|\kappa_{\mathcal B}-U_A\\|_{\mathrm{TV}} \mid\text{pre-call history}\big]\longrightarrow0.$$ The assertion is uniform in the starting cyclic phase and in the initial base placement. The multiplier law is independent of the incoming overlay permutation.
  - Explanation: A call with many phases of preparation and active sweeps with extra switches makes the multiplier on one group of indices nearly uniform in expected total variation, conditional on the whole base shuffle.
- **claim-transverse-rounds** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-transverse-rounds)): Let $R_1,\ldots,R_K$ and $C_1,\ldots,C_K$ partition $V$, with $\|R_i\cap C_j\|=n/K^2$. Independently shuffle uniformly within every $R$-group, then within every $C$-group, and then within every $R$-group again. For the fixed $K=8$ and $n=2^d$, the resulting permutation law has total-variation distance $o(1)$ from uniform on $\mathfrak S(V)$ as $d\to\infty$.
  - Explanation: Uniform shuffles within the row groups, then the column groups, then the row groups again, give a permutation law near uniform on the whole index set. The proof reduces to a local limit estimate for contingency tables.
- **claim-two-half-mixing** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-two-halves)): Set $L=2^{23}$, $p=1-1/(2L)$, and choose $b$ by <a href="#eq:tabloid-parameters" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:tabloid-parameters">[eq:tabloid-parameters]</a>. For $n=2^d$, the full-deck law from any deterministic start approaches uniform in total variation after $400bd$ physical shuffles.
  - Explanation: Splitting the run into two independent blocks, with inverse-image caps for the first and a conditional product for the second, yields full-deck mixing after an explicit multiple of d physical shuffles.
- **claim-color-product** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-color-product)): For every feasible occupancy history $Z=z$, all mixed-color switch values are fixed and all same-color switch values are independent fair bits. Choose $t_z$ to send the increasing list of $A$ to the increasing list of its endpoint color set and likewise for $C$. Then $$W=t_z(Y_A\times Y_C),$$ where, conditional on $Z=z$, $Y_A\in S_A$ and $Y_C\in S_C$ are independent. Their conditional laws depend only on $z$.
  - Explanation: Given the occupancy history of the two colors, mixed-color switches are fixed and same-color switches are independent fair bits. The block permutation factors as a deterministic relabeling times two conditionally independent half-permutations.

```json
    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-slot-kernel",
        "exact_quote": "Fix a deterministic starting layout and a set of outside labels. For any feasible specification $e$ of their paths through a finite interval, conditional on $e$ all switch values on edges incident to an outside card are fixed, and all other switch values are independent fair bits. Let $F_s$ be the set of free positions at boundary $s$, and let $m=|F_s|$. Write $\\iota_s:[m]\\to F_s$ for the increasing bijection. Every completion of the unexposed bits induces a bijection $T_e:F_0\\to F_T$, hence a permutation $$g_e=\\iota_T^{-1}T_e\\iota_0\\in S_m.$$ Its conditional law depends on the outside paths and positions, but not on the incoming assignment of free labels. On consecutive intervals the corresponding permutations multiply with later intervals on the left. Conditional on all the outside paths, their remaining switch arrays are independent, so their conditional laws compose by convolution.",
        "source_id": "PM-238.5"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-slot-kernel",
          "exact_quote": "Fix a deterministic starting layout and a set of outside labels. Every completion of the unexposed bits induces a bijection $T_e:F_0\\to F_T$, hence a permutation $$g_e=\\iota_T^{-1}T_e\\iota_0\\in S_m.$$ Its conditional law depends on the outside paths and positions, but not on the incoming assignment of free labels. On consecutive intervals the corresponding permutations multiply with later intervals on the left. Conditional on all the outside paths, their remaining switch arrays are independent, so their conditional laws compose by convolution.",
          "source_id": "PM-238.5"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Fix a deterministic starting layout and a set of outside labels.         "anchor": "stmt-lem-coarse-block-estimates",
        "exact_quote": "For the one-sweep matrix $Q$ defined above and every free-slot row vector $u$, a good starting blocked set $E$ satisfies $$\\mathbb E\\|uQ\\|^2=u A_E u^\\top\n \\le \\|u_{\\rm c}\\|^2+q^{d-h}\\|u\\|^2. \\label{special:burn-active:eq:2}$$ For every starting blocked set $E$ with $|E|\\le n/2$, the ending set satisfies $$\\mathbb P(E'\\text{ not good})\\le\n \\epsilon_n. \\label{special:burn-active:eq:3}$$ If $u$ has sum zero, then its coarse part on the ending free slots satisfies $$\\mathbb E\\|(uQ)_{\\rm c}\\|^2\\le (4\\cdot 2^{-h}+\\epsilon_n)\\|u\\|^2. \\label{special:burn-active:eq:4}$$",

        "exact_quote": "<span id=\"lem:character-fixed-points\" label=\"lem:character-fixed-points\"></span>Fix $0<\\delta<1/4$. <a id="pm-s2"></a>
```
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **sweep** ([source](#pm-h-2)): A *sweep* visits all $d$ directions once and costs $d$ physical shuffles.
- **layer** ([source](#sec:conditional)): A layer means one such update; a sweep consists of $d$ consecutive layers containing all directions.
- **total variation convention** ([source](#pm-h-2)): Write $U_m$ for uniform measure on the symmetric group $S_m$, and use total variation with its factor $1/2$.
- **mixing time** ([source](#pm-h-2)): The mixing time $t_{\mathrm{mix}}(d)$ is the least number of physical shuffles at which the full-deck law is within $1/4$ of $U_n$ from every deterministic start.
- **increasing slot index map** ([source](#stmt-lem-slot-kernel)): Write $\iota_s:[m]\to F_s$ for the increasing bijection.
- **coarse blocks** ([source](#sec:preparation)): Let $h=\lfloor d/2\rfloor$, and call the blocks specified by bits $>h$ the coarse blocks (size $2^h$).
- **good blocked set** ([source](#sec:preparation)): Call a blocked set good if its fraction in every coarse block is at most $3/4$.
- **poor carrier** ([source](#pm-h-7)): Call the carrier poor if this count is less than $d/4$.
- **indices of the base shuffle** ([source](#pm-h-19)): Call the initial position names of the base shuffle its **indices**, in $V$.
- **call on a group** ([source](#pm-h-19)): For a fixed group $A\subseteq V$ of $q$ indices, we call the following procedure a *call* on $A$.
- **occupancy history** ([source](#pm-h-25)): Let $Z$ record the set of positions occupied by each color after every layer, including the initial and final boundary.

| q-reset-time | physical shuffles for full-deck mixing in the reset construction | ≤ | $171798691968d$ | d tends to infinity |
| q-reset-mass-contraction | expected squared norm of signed hole masses after two sweeps, relative to the initial squared norm L | ≤ | $N^{-\rho/16}L$ | hole count at least a fixed fraction of N |
| q-reset-partial-eps | total variation error of partial mixing at fixed omitted fraction | = | $N^{-8}$ | tuple size at most (1-rho)N |
| q-reset-minorization-p0 | minorization constant of the two-step reset kernel | = | $1-2\varepsilon_N$ | b at most (1-rho)N |
| q-overlay-time | physical shuffles for full-deck mixing in the overlay construction | ≤ | $33030144d$ | d tends to infinity |
| q-two-halves-time | physical shuffles for full-deck mixing in the two-half construction | ≤ | $400bd$ | b fixed by the tabloid parameter formula |

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-slot-kernel** [claim](#stmt-lem-slot-kernel) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-slot-kernel): Cylinder conditioning on the outside paths, then bijection of free inputs and outputs and composition across intervals. -> conditional permutation of free slots composing by convolution
- **step-coarse-estimates** [claim](#stmt-lem-coarse-block-estimates) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-coarse-block-estimates): Two-copy collision kernel, coarse-block decomposition, and a negative dependence bound for blocked slots. -> fluctuation contraction, small coarse energy, and bad-set probability bound
- **step-half-preparation** [claim](#stmt-prop-half-preparation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-half-preparation): Iterate the two-sweep recursion for the squared norm and sum next-card deviations sequentially. -> uniform ordered half-deck positions within a polynomial error
- **step-character-bound** [claim](#stmt-input-character) / [proof](#proof-stmt-input-character): Apply the uniform Larsen-Shalev bound, then restrict to the subgroup fixing the fixed points via branching. -> normalized character bounds in terms of fixed points
- **step-degree-sum** [claim](#stmt-lem-degree-sum) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-degree-sum): Count standard Young tableaux for long first rows and columns, then bound the remaining shapes exponentially. -> vanishing reciprocal-square degree sum
- **step-conditional-mixing** [claim](#stmt-thm-methods) / [proof](#sec:burn-completion): Chunks of preparation and active sweeps, truncation and character trace bounds, Plancherel, then coupling of outside half-decks and free orders. -> conditional uniformity and full-deck mixing in the stated number of shuffles
- **step-reset-mass-contraction** [claim](#stmt-lem-reset-mass-contraction) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-reset-mass-contraction): Block moment recursion with density control by a Chernoff bound, telescoping the correction terms. -> two-sweep polynomial contraction of squared norm
- **step-reset-partial-mixing** [claim](#stmt-prop-reset-partial-mixing) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-reset-partial-mixing): Apply mass contraction repeatedly per label, then telescope the total variation over the tuple. -> partial mixing at fixed omitted fraction in both coordinate orders
- **step-reset-minorization** [claim](#stmt-prop-reset-minorization) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-reset-minorization): Write the square kernel as a product with the transpose kernel and truncate each factor at the uniform value. -> pointwise minorization of the two-step kernel
- **step-reset-conditional** [claim](#stmt-prop-reset-conditional) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-reset-conditional): Central relative increments, minorization splitting, goodness marks, character return estimate, and a Cauchy-Schwarz comparison with uniform. -> expected conditional total variation tends to zero
- **step-reset-full-deck** [claim](#stmt-thm-reset) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-reset-conditional-2): Combine nonhole marginal closeness with conditional hole uniformity and count the physical shuffles. -> full-deck mixing after the stated multiple of d shuffles
- **step-overlay-preparation** [claim](#stmt-lem-overlay-preparation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-overlay-preparation): Randomized linear change of frame so directions are fresh, then a recursive squared-norm contraction for each card. -> near-uniform ordered positions of a fraction of base cards
- **step-overlay-call** [claim](#stmt-prop-overlay-call) / [proof](#sec:overlay-ideal): Ideal independent segments, encounter truncation, character estimates, and Plancherel for the convolved multipliers. -> conditional group multiplier near uniform
- **step-transverse-rounds** [claim](#stmt-lem-transverse-rounds) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-transverse-rounds): Reduce to count tables, then a local limit theorem for contingency tables via Stirling expansion and smoothing. -> three-round row-column-row law close to uniform
- **step-overlay-full-deck** [claim](#stmt-thm-overlay) / [proof](#special:overlay:bound): Sum the per-call errors over the calls in the three rounds and count the layers. -> full-deck mixing after the stated multiple of d shuffles
- **step-color-product** [claim](#stmt-lem-color-product) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-color-product): The occupancy history is a cylinder event in the switch array; apply the slot-bijection construction to each color separately. -> independent internal half-permutations
- **step-two-halves** [claim](#stmt-thm-two-halves) / [proof](#pm-h-26): Truncate for caps, apply the conditional product transfer, then Plancherel for a hundred-fold convolution and count the physical shuffles. -> full-deck mixing after the stated multiple of d shuffles

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **inv-first-route-time**: The first route reaches full-deck mixing after a number of physical shuffles that is an explicit integer multiple of d, obtained from the preparation length, the number of chunks and one active sweep per chunk.
- **inv-reset-time**: The reset construction gives an explicit integer multiple of d physical shuffles, computed from the number of blocks and the sweep count of one reset block.
- **inv-overlay-time**: The overlay construction gives an explicit integer multiple of d physical shuffles, computed from the number of calls, the number of phases and the layers per phase.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-small-deck-constants**: Quote the explicit multiples of d as concrete shuffle counts for a small or fixed deck. -> The statements only assert that the total-variation distance tends to zero as d grows; the arithmetic of the constants says nothing about a finite deck.
- **anti-non-power-of-two**: Apply the computed time constants to a deck whose size is not a power of two. -> The cyclic coordinate structure of the cube is unavailable, so the time formulas have no meaning there.
- **anti-constant-as-sharp**: Treat the three computed constants as the true mixing time or compare them as sharp rates. -> Each is only an upper bound from its own proof route, so differences between them reflect proof technique.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `constructive`. Only the finite arithmetic that produces the explicit shuffle-count multipliers of d in Theorems 1, 7 and 12 is a finite procedure; the probabilistic convergence statements themselves are asymptotic and are not tested.

```python
# INPUTS: one line: 'first s0 J' or 'reset k l' (omitted fraction 2^-k, l blocks) or 'overlay K k'; all non-negative integers.
# PRECONDITIONS: tokens well formed; for reset the sweep count is ceil(320 * 2^k) exactly.
# OUTPUTS: one integer, the multiplier of d in the physical shuffle count.
# TIME: constant number of big-integer operations.
# SPACE: constant.
# ARITHMETIC: exact Python integers.
# FAILURE: unknown kind or wrong token count exits with a nonzero status.
import sys

def main():
    parts = sys.stdin.read().split()
    kind = parts[0]
    a = [int(x) for x in parts[1:]]
    if kind == "first":
        s0, J = a
        out = s0 + J * (s0 + 1)
    elif kind == "reset":
        k, l = a
        s = 320 * 2**k
        out = l * (4 * s + 1)
    elif kind == "overlay":
        K, k = a
        out = 3 * K * k * 21
    else:
        sys.exit(2)
    print(out)

main()
```

<a id="pm-s5"></a>
## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The headline theorem is an asymptotic statement about a total-variation limit as d grows. It gives no executable procedure and no finite error bound for a concrete deck size, so there is nothing to implement as a library. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The overlay result needs a physical-shuffle count that is a huge multiple of d and applies only to power-of-two decks. It gives no usable guidance for dealing or shuffling in a real card game. |
| tools | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The only reusable analytic ingredient is the reciprocal-degree summability estimate for symmetric-group representations. It is a proof lemma, not a tool or checker that a user could run. |
| algorithms | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The slot-kernel lemma describes a conditional random bijection obtained by revealing paths. It is a probabilistic structure used inside proofs, with no stated step-by-step algorithm and no complexity claim. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The colour-occupancy history and the factorised internal permutations are analysis devices. The paper proposes no stored representation or query operations. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The reset construction requires a physical-shuffle count that is an enormous multiple of d. A fixed-depth switching fabric built on it would be impractical, and the paper discusses no hardware. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The two-half result gives only an asymptotic statement with a very large constant in a power-of-two setting. It supplies no security reduction, concrete round count or adversary model, so it cannot justify a cipher. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The character bound on symmetric groups is pure representation theory used to control Fourier coefficients. It has no learning problem, model or data attached. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The partial-mixing proposition bounds a distance to uniform for tuples of labels. It poses no objective function, constraint set or search procedure. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The overlay call applies extra switches to a group of base indices inside one synchronous shuffle. There are no nodes, messages, faults or asynchrony in the model. |
| other | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The nearest wider use is as a probability and mixing-time tool for conditional permutation laws. That is a theoretical contribution, not an engineering construction, and its blocked uses are those of the main theorem. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-power-of-two-deck** (hypothesis): The reset construction is stated only for decks of size $n=2^d$ and is proved only as d grows. Its coordinate structure comes from the binary cube, so the result does not extend to other deck sizes. Blocked use: ['Quoting the reset time bound for a deck whose size is not a power of two', 'Quoting the reset time bound as a finite-size guarantee for a small fixed d']
- **limit-asymptotic-overlay-constant** (approximation): The overlay result asserts only that the total-variation distance tends to zero after an explicit multiple of d shuffles. It supplies no error bound at any finite d, and the multiple is an upper bound from one proof route, not a sharp rate. Blocked use: ['Using the stated multiple of d as a certified shuffle budget for a concrete finite deck', 'Comparing the stated constants of the different constructions as if they were true mixing times']
- **limit-half-deck-fraction** (hypothesis): The preparation estimate covers ordered lists of at most half of the labels and holds only for all sufficiently large d. Lists that exceed half the deck are outside the statement. Blocked use: ['Applying the polynomial total-variation bound to a list covering more than half of the deck', 'Applying the bound at a small dimension d before the large-d threshold is known']
- **limit-average-not-pointwise** (formal_scope): The conditional uniformity is an expectation over the ordinary law of the outside paths. The paper explicitly does not assert a bound for each fixed path environment. Blocked use: ['Claiming that every revealed path environment leaves the remaining assignment near uniform', 'Using the conditional result as a worst-case guarantee against an adversary who chooses the environment']
- **limit-hole-density** (hypothesis): The two-sweep mass contraction needs the holes to occupy at least a fixed positive fraction of the positions, and the threshold on d depends only on that fraction. When the free set is sparser, the contraction is not guaranteed. Blocked use: ['Applying the contraction when almost all positions are occupied by revealed labels', 'Applying the contraction with a fraction that shrinks with d']
- **limit-deterministic-schedule** (edge_case): The estimates rely on the deterministic cyclic schedule of coordinate directions. The paper notes that a random-key chain differs from this schedule and needs its own arguments. Blocked use: ['Transferring the bounds to shuffles with key-dependent or adaptively chosen directions', 'Transferring the bounds to non-cyclic direction orders without a new proof']
- **limit-external-inputs** (formal_scope): The two-half construction depends on two separately proved companion results, whose hypotheses must hold exactly as stated. The present paper does not reprove them. Blocked use: ['Citing the two-half bound without checking the conditions of both companion theorems', 'Treating the two-half argument as self-contained']
- **limit-maximal-coupling-nonconstructive** (nonconstructive): The final coupling step uses maximal couplings, whose existence is invoked without an explicit construction. The argument therefore yields no sampling procedure for the coupled decks. Blocked use: ['Presenting the coupling as an implementable algorithm for generating coupled shuffles', 'Deriving a running time for the coupling step']

<a id="pm-formal"></a>
### Formal statement (Lean 4)

Corpus status: formalized in the source corpus (toolchain `leanprover/lean4:v4.34.1`, Mathlib `d13f23b723b8a846827a245b89c10fc7d3f11612`). Not re-checked here; the statements give the exact quantifiers and hypotheses, not a proof.

Scope note of family 238 (`lean/docs/238.md`, verbatim):

> The following describes the scope of the Lean formalization related to the following accompanying paper(s):
>
> - [Optimal-order mixing of the Thorp shuffle](../../preprints/Optimal-order-mixing-of-the-Thorp-shuffle-September-26-2026/paper.pdf)
> - [From partial permutation information to Fourier bounds](../../preprints/From-partial-permutation-information-to-Fourier-bounds-September-26-2026/main.pdf)
> - [Conditional permutations in a revealed switching environment](../../preprints/Conditional-permutations-in-a-revealed-switching-environment-September-26-2026/paper.pdf)
> - [Routing densities and representation contraction for Thorp sweeps](../../preprints/Routing-densities-and-representation-contraction-for-Thorp-sweeps-September-26-2026/paper.pdf)
> - [Row–column symmetry and contraction of coordinate sweeps](../../preprints/Row-column-symmetry-and-contraction-of-coordinate-sweeps-September-26-2026/paper.pdf)
> - [Random-subspace tests and trace smoothing for coordinate sweeps](../../preprints/Random-subspace-tests-and-trace-smoothing-for-coordinate-sweeps-September-26-2026/paper.pdf)
> - [Compatibility entropy and the spectrum of a Thorp sweep](../../preprints/Compatibility-entropy-and-the-spectrum-of-a-Thorp-sweep-September-26-2026/paper.pdf)
> - [Signed tensor densities and diagram budgets for the Thorp shuffle](../../preprints/Signed-tensor-densities-and-diagram-budgets-for-coordinate-sweeps-September-26-2026/paper.pdf)
> - [Conditional coordinate sweeps and analytic transfer](../../preprints/Conditional-coordinate-sweeps-and-analytic-transfer-September-26-2026/main.pdf)
> - [A strict four-row permanent inequality and permutation moments](../../preprints/A-strict-four-row-permanent-inequality-and-permutation-moments-September-26-2026/main.pdf)
>
> ## Scope
>
> The formalization proves optimal-order mixing of the Thorp shuffle on $2^d$ cards. After $1600d$ complete shuffles, the full permutation law converges in total variation to uniform as $d\to\infty$, uniformly over initial decks. Together with the support lower bound of $2d-O(1)$, this gives mixing time $\Theta(d)=\Theta(\log(2^d))$.
>
> The linked supporting results include frame and conditional-list estimates, regular trace and spectral bounds, signed moments, and full-density $L^2$ control. They also bound reciprocal Specht-dimension sums and give the eight-block Fourier estimate used in the mixing argument.
>
> The formalized supporting result turns bounds on partial-permutation cosets into Fourier bounds. Partition a finite set into $b\ge1$ nonempty blocks, and let $f\ge0$ be a subprobability weight on its symmetric group whose left-coset masses for each block subgroup are at most $B$. For every irreducible unitary representation of dimension $D$ and every $u>0$, both the squared Hilbert–Schmidt norm and squared operator norm of its Fourier transform are at most $bB\,C(u)D^{-1+(u+2)/b}$, where $C(u)$ is the stated symmetric-group degree constant.
>
> The paper's asymptotic conclusion about the product of two random permutations is outside this selected finite estimate.
>
> The formalized results give averaged conditional mixing for half-permutations in the revealed-path model and full-deck mixing for the Thorp shuffle. In particular, the full-deck total-variation distance tends to zero after $16040400d$ steps on $2^d$ cards, uniformly over deterministic initial decks. The conditional estimate is averaged over the actual outside-path law, rather than asserted for each individual environment. The formalization also includes the associated overlay, reset minorization, and two-color conditional constructions.
>
> The formalization covers nine routing and representation estimates for Thorp sweeps: adaptive operator and fourth-trace bounds, Casimir moments, dense truncation, sparse contact, harmonic-cycle contraction, smoothing, and three tail or sparse-saving estimates. They bound routing-density deviations and Fourier operators in dense and sparse regimes, including exponential savings in the level scale and representation dimension.
>
> The conditional high-height estimate requires a sufficiently large cutoff height $J$ that exceeds twice the number $s$ of coordinates in the fixed low block, so $J>2s$. These are the earlier nine statements associated with the paper; its changed later statements and six other result blocks are outside this scope.
>
> The formalization proves uniform representation bounds for one coordinate sweep of the Thorp shuffle. For each irreducible representation of dimension $D$, it chooses a weight between $D^{3/4}$ and $D$ and a Schatten exponent bounded by one absolute constant so that the weighted Schatten moment is at most one. The sweep's operator norm is consequently at most $D^{-c}$ for one absolute $c>0$.
>
> The supporting row–column estimate bounds the total squared overlap over all multiplicity copies on an occupied board, with explicit dependence on the row and column representation dimensions, the global dimension, the number of missing cells, and the number of rows or columns containing the global Young diagram. The paper's full physical-mixing conclusion is outside these selected representation estimates.
>
> The formalization proves a uniform trace-smoothing estimate for coordinate sweeps of the Thorp shuffle on $2^d$ cards. One absolute sweep parameter works for every $d\ge1$: the regular trace is at most $1+(2^d)^{-10}$, and the full permutation law after the corresponding fixed number of sweeps is within $\tfrac12(2^d)^{-5}$ of uniform in total variation, for every initial deck. Thus the selected upper bound uses $O(d)$ physical shuffles.
>
> The formalized result is the weighted compatibility theorem for balanced $A\times D$ rectangles, with $n=AD$ and $\sqrt n/2\le A,D\le2\sqrt n$. For arbitrary nonnegative weights on the row and column permutation groups, the normalized compatibility average is bounded by $\exp(C_0n^{54/100})$ times the product of the marginal $L^{1/\theta}$ factors, where $\theta=1-L/\log\sqrt n>0$ and the constants are absolute. Compatibility means injectivity in every original column. The bounded regular-moment and other moment/rank conclusions are not included.
>
> The formalization gives signed representation estimates for coordinate sweeps of the Thorp shuffle. For every signed occurrence of a Young diagram of size $2^d$, it bounds the logarithm of a fixed-order weighted sweep moment by a small multiple of the signed partition entropy plus an explicit remainder-size budget. The moment order is uniform over the diagrams and decompositions after the two small coefficients are fixed.
>
> The linked supporting angle bound controls the overlap of row, column, and global signed-type projections on an occupied rectangle, with explicit entropy, dimension, and missing-cell factors. These selected estimates support the paper's full-density argument; the full $L^2$ and total-variation mixing conclusion is outside them.
>
> The formalized binary-sweep theorem gives a universal power contraction in every irreducible representation: for sufficiently large $d$, the averaged sweep on $2^d$ slots has operator norm at most $D^{-g}$ in representation dimension $D$, for some $g>0$. The sign expectation is zero, and a fixed number of independent sweeps approaches uniform in total variation from every initial deck. A separate conditional moment estimate covers allowed power-of-two grids and disjoint coordinate-respecting trajectories, with its stated feasibility premise. Physical-shuffle law identification is not included.
>
> The formalization proves the paper's strict four-row permanent inequality. There are constants $4/3<p<2$ and $\varepsilon>0$ such that every probability law on $S_4$ within total-variation distance $\varepsilon$ of uniform, and with exactly uniform coordinate marginals, satisfies the permanent bound by the product of the four $L^p$ row norms for every nonnegative matrix. The exponent and neighborhood are uniform over those laws. The Thorp mixing consequence is outside this selected statement.

```lean
import Mathlib

namespace OAI

/-!
Conditional moments for one ordered coordinate sweep on power-of-two rectangular
grids. The line law mixes uniform permutations with a binary coordinate sweep.
The trace is unnormalized, and the trajectory cost uses falling factorials.
-/

noncomputable section

open scoped BigOperators Matrix.Norms.L2Operator ComplexOrder ComplexConjugate MatrixOrder ENNReal
open MeasureTheory
attribute [local instance] Classical.propDecidable

namespace CoordinateSweeps

structure FiniteLaw (Ω : Type*) where
  mass : Ω → ℝ
  nonneg : ∀ x, 0 ≤ mass x
  total : ∑ᶠ x, mass x = 1

namespace FiniteLaw

variable {Ω X Y : Type*}

instance : CoeFun (FiniteLaw Ω) (fun _ => Ω → ℝ) := ⟨FiniteLaw.mass⟩

@[ext] theorem ext {μ ν : FiniteLaw Ω} (h : ∀ x, μ x = ν x) : μ = ν := by
  cases μ
  cases ν
  congr
  exact funext h

variable [Fintype Ω] [Fintype X] [Fintype Y]

@[simp] theorem sum_mass (μ : FiniteLaw Ω) : ∑ x, μ x = 1 := by
  simpa only [finsum_eq_sum_of_fintype] using μ.total

def uniform (Ω : Type*) [Fintype Ω] [Nonempty Ω] : FiniteLaw Ω where
  mass _ := (Fintype.card Ω : ℝ)⁻¹
  nonneg _ := inv_nonneg.mpr (Nat.cast_nonneg _)
  total := by
    rw [finsum_eq_sum_of_fintype]
    simp only [Finset.sum_const, Finset.card_univ, nsmul_eq_mul]
    exact mul_inv_cancel₀ (by exact_mod_cast Fintype.card_ne_zero)

def map (μ : FiniteLaw Ω) (f : Ω → X) : FiniteLaw X where
  mass y := ∑ x, if f x = y then μ x else 0
  nonneg y := Finset.sum_nonneg (fun x _ => by split_ifs; exact μ.nonneg x; exact le_rfl)
  total := by
    rw [finsum_eq_sum_of_fintype]
    classical
    rw [Finset.sum_comm]
    simp [μ.sum_mass]

end FiniteLaw

abbrev Cube (d : ℕ) := Fin d → Bool

def pairSwitch {L : Type*} (bits : L → Bool) : Equiv.Perm (Bool × L) where
  toFun x := (Bool.xor x.1 (bits x.2), x.2)
  invFun x := (Bool.xor x.1 (bits x.2), x.2)
  left_inv point := by
    rcases point with ⟨bit, label⟩
    change ((bit ^^ bits label) ^^ bits label, label) = (bit, label)
    cases bit <;> cases bits label <;> simp
  right_inv point := by
    rcases point with ⟨bit, label⟩
    change ((bit ^^ bits label) ^^ bits label, label) = (bit, label)
    cases bit <;> cases bits label <;> simp

def binaryLayer {d : ℕ} (j : Fin d) (bits : ({k : Fin d // k ≠ j} → Bool) → Bool) :
    Equiv.Perm (Cube d) :=
  (Equiv.funSplitAt j Bool).trans ((pairSwitch bits).trans (Equiv.funSplitAt j Bool).symm)

/-- Coordinate zero acts first. -/
def orderedProduct {G : Type*} [Monoid G] {d : ℕ} (g : Fin d → G) : G :=
  (List.ofFn g).reverse.prod

abbrev BinaryChoices (d : ℕ) := ∀ j : Fin d, ({k : Fin d // k ≠ j} → Bool) → Bool

instance (d : ℕ) : Nonempty (BinaryChoices d) := ⟨fun _ _ => false⟩

def binarySweep (d : ℕ) (bits : BinaryChoices d) : Equiv.Perm (Cube d) :=
  orderedProduct (fun j => binaryLayer j (bits j))

def binaryLaw (d : ℕ) : FiniteLaw (Equiv.Perm (Cube d)) :=
  (FiniteLaw.uniform (BinaryChoices d)).map (binarySweep d)

structure Grid where
  b : ℕ
  positive : 0 < b
  bits : Fin b → ℕ

namespace Grid

def size (G : Grid) : ℕ := ∏ j, 2 ^ G.bits j

abbrev Slot (G : Grid) := ∀ j, Cube (G.bits j)

abbrev Line (G : Grid) (j : Fin G.b) := ∀ k : {k : Fin G.b // k ≠ j}, Cube (G.bits k)

abbrev Choices (G : Grid) := ∀ j, G.Line j → Equiv.Perm (Cube (G.bits j))

def fiberPerm {A B : Type*} (p : B → Equiv.Perm A) : Equiv.Perm (A × B) where
  toFun x := (p x.2 x.1, x.2)
  invFun x := ((p x.2).symm x.1, x.2)
  left_inv x := by simp
  right_inv x := by simp

def stage (G : Grid) (ω : G.Choices) (j : Fin G.b) : Equiv.Perm G.Slot :=
  (Equiv.piSplitAt j (fun k => Cube (G.bits k))).trans
    ((fiberPerm (ω j)).trans (Equiv.piSplitAt j (fun k => Cube (G.bits k))).symm)

def boundary (G : Grid) (ω : G.Choices) (t : ℕ) : Equiv.Perm G.Slot :=
  ((List.ofFn (G.stage ω)).take t).reverse.prod

def sweep (G : Grid) (ω : G.Choices) : Equiv.Perm G.Slot := G.boundary ω G.b

/-- The side lengths are in `[R, R²]`, where the single global scale is `R = 2^r`. -/
def Allowed (G : Grid) (r : ℕ) : Prop := ∀ j, r ≤ G.bits j ∧ G.bits j ≤ 2 * r

/-- Full disjoint paths, including every boundary of the ordered sweep. -/
structure Holes (G : Grid) (h : ℕ) where
  path : Fin h → Fin (G.b + 1) → G.Slot
  disjoint : ∀ t, Function.Injective (fun i => path i t)
  coordinate_step : ∀ i (j : Fin G.b) (k : Fin G.b), k ≠ j →
    path i j.succ k = path i j.castSucc k

namespace Holes

variable {G : Grid} {h : ℕ}

def Compatible (H : G.Holes h) (ω : G.Choices) : Prop :=
  ∀ i (t : Fin (G.b + 1)), G.boundary ω t (H.path i 0) = H.path i t

def Feasible (H : G.Holes h) : Prop := ∃ ω, H.Compatible ω

def lineCount (H : G.Holes h) (j : Fin G.b) (L : G.Line j) : ℕ :=
  (Finset.univ.filter (fun i : Fin h =>
    (fun k : {k : Fin G.b // k ≠ j} => H.path i j.castSucc k) = L)).card

def cost (H : G.Holes h) : ℝ :=
  ∑ j, ∑ L : G.Line j,
    Real.log (((2 ^ G.bits j : ℕ) : ℝ) ^ H.lineCount j L /
      ((2 ^ G.bits j).descFactorial (H.lineCount j L) : ℝ))

/-- The symmetric group on the unmarked input sites. -/
def stabilizer (H : G.Holes h) : Subgroup (Equiv.Perm G.Slot) where
  carrier g := ∀ i, g (H.path i 0) = H.path i 0
  one_mem' _ := rfl
  mul_mem' := by
    intro g g' hg hg' i
    change g (g' (H.path i 0)) = H.path i 0
    rw [hg' i, hg i]
  inv_mem' hg i := (Equiv.symm_apply_eq _).mpr (hg i).symm

def reference (H : G.Holes h) (hf : H.Feasible) : G.Choices := hf.choose

theorem reference_compatible (H : G.Holes h) (hf : H.Feasible) :
    H.Compatible (H.reference hf) := hf.choose_spec

/-- A reference completion identifies the free input and output sites. -/
def residual (H : G.Holes h) (hf : H.Feasible) (ω : {ω : G.Choices // H.Compatible ω}) :
    H.stabilizer :=
  ⟨(G.sweep (H.reference hf))⁻¹ * G.sweep ω.val, by
    intro i
    change (G.sweep (H.reference hf)).symm (G.sweep ω.val (H.path i 0)) = H.path i 0
    apply (Equiv.symm_apply_eq _).mpr
    exact (ω.property i (Fin.last _)).trans ((H.reference_compatible hf i (Fin.last _)).symm)⟩

end Holes

/-- Independent line permutations with law `(1-z) U + z B`. -/
def choiceWeight (G : Grid) (z : ℝ) (ω : G.Choices) : ℝ :=
  ∏ j, ∏ L : G.Line j,
    ((1-z) * (Fintype.card (Equiv.Perm (Cube (G.bits j))) : ℝ)⁻¹ +
      z * binaryLaw (G.bits j) (ω j L))

namespace Holes

variable {G : Grid} {h : ℕ}

def probability (H : G.Holes h) (z : ℝ) : ℝ :=
  ∑ ω : {ω : G.Choices // H.Compatible ω}, G.choiceWeight z ω.val

end Holes

def eventDistance (G : Grid) (E : Finset G.Choices) (z : ℝ) : ℝ :=
  ∑ ω ∈ E, |G.choiceWeight z ω/(∑ v ∈ E, G.choiceWeight z v)-
    G.choiceWeight 0 ω/(∑ v ∈ E, G.choiceWeight 0 v)|

end Grid

structure UnitaryIrrep (Γ : Type*) [Group Γ] where
  dimension : ℕ
  positive : 0 < dimension
  matrix : Γ →* Matrix (Fin dimension) (Fin dimension) ℂ
  unitary : ∀ g, (matrix g).conjTranspose * matrix g = 1
  irreducible : ∀ S : Submodule ℂ (Fin dimension → ℂ),
    (∀ g v, v ∈ S → (matrix g).mulVec v ∈ S) → S = ⊥ ∨ S = ⊤

/-- Unnormalized Schatten `2q` moment, without division by the dimension. -/
def schattenMoment {D : ℕ} (q : ℕ) (K : Matrix (Fin D) (Fin D) ℂ) : ℝ :=
  (Matrix.trace ((K.conjTranspose * K) ^ q)).re

namespace Grid.Holes

variable {G : Grid} {h : ℕ}

def conditionalAverage (H : G.Holes h) (hf : H.Feasible) (z : ℝ)
    (ρ : UnitaryIrrep H.stabilizer) : Matrix (Fin ρ.dimension) (Fin ρ.dimension) ℂ :=
  (H.probability z : ℂ)⁻¹ •
    ∑ ω : {ω : G.Choices // H.Compatible ω},
      (G.choiceWeight z ω.val : ℂ) • ρ.matrix (H.residual hf ω)

end Grid.Holes

def a : ℝ := 1 / 100
def c0 : ℝ := a / 100
def e0 : ℝ := a / 100
def c (s : ℕ) : ℝ := c0 + 1 / Real.sqrt (Real.log s)
def e (s : ℕ) : ℝ := e0 - 1 / Real.sqrt (Real.log s)

/-- The logarithm of a zero moment is minus infinity. -/
def logMoment (t : ℝ) : EReal := if t = 0 then ⊥ else (Real.log t : EReal)

def ConditionalMain : Prop :=
  ∃ (r q : ℕ) (zStar : ℝ), 1 ≤ r ∧ 1 ≤ q ∧ 0 < zStar ∧ zStar ≤ 1 / ((2 : ℕ) : ℝ) ∧
    ∀ (G : Grid), G.Allowed r → ∀ (h : ℕ) (H : G.Holes h) (hf : H.Feasible),
    ∀ z ∈ Set.Icc 0 zStar, ∀ ρ : UnitaryIrrep H.stabilizer,
      logMoment (schattenMoment q (H.conditionalAverage hf z ρ)) ≤
        ((-c G.size * Real.log ρ.dimension + e G.size * h * Real.log G.size - H.cost : ℝ) : EReal)

def MomentBound (G : Grid) (q : ℕ) (z : ℝ) : Prop :=
  ∀ (h : ℕ) (H : G.Holes h) (hf : H.Feasible) (σ : UnitaryIrrep H.stabilizer),
    schattenMoment q (H.conditionalAverage hf z σ) ≤
      Real.exp (-c G.size*Real.log σ.dimension+e G.size*h*Real.log G.size-H.cost)

def ScaleLarge (r : ℕ) : Prop :=
  1 ≤ r ∧ (2/e0)^2 ≤ (r : ℝ)*Real.log 2 ∧ 4/e0 ≤ (r : ℝ)*Real.log 2

def baseWidth (r : ℕ) : ℝ := ((2^(2*r)).factorial : ℕ)
def baseZeta (r : ℕ) : ℝ := 1/(4*(baseWidth r)^2)

namespace ScalarInduction

structure MainSizeReady (q : ℕ) (s : ℝ) : Prop where
  one : 1 ≤ Real.log s
  sparse : (100004:ℝ)*s^(1-a/4) ≤ s^(1-(1:ℝ)/1600)
  hook : (((2*q+1:ℕ):ℝ)*4+82*c0)*2*s^((4:ℝ)/5+2*a)*Real.log (s+1) ≤ s^((9:ℝ)/10)
  removed : s^((9:ℝ)/10)+s^(1-a/2)*Real.log s ≤ s^(1-a/4)/(20*Real.sqrt (Real.log s))

end ScalarInduction

namespace SparseScalar

def theta : ℝ := 1/1600
def K0 : ℝ := 100000
def B0 : ℕ := 12800
def xi : ℝ := 1/2048000000
def rho : ℝ := 1/32768000000
def q : ℕ := 65536000000

def uniformRate : ℝ := Real.log ((2 : ℕ) : ℝ) + (B0 : ℝ) *
  (K0 + 1 + Real.log ((4 : ℕ) : ℝ) +
    Real.log (((16 : ℕ) : ℝ) * Real.exp ((2 : ℕ) : ℝ)) +
    Real.log (1 + ((16 : ℕ) : ℝ) * Real.exp ((2 : ℕ) : ℝ)))

structure SizeReady (s : ℝ) : Prop where
  two : 2 ≤ s
  logpow : Real.log s ≤ s^(theta/4)
  powtwo : 2 ≤ s^(theta/4)
  powB : (B0+1:ℝ) ≤ s^(theta/2)
  count : 4/s^(1/(4*(B0:ℝ))) ≤ theta/(64*B0)
  absorb : Real.log (1+2*(B0:ℝ))+Real.log 2 ≤ xi*Real.log s
  eta : s^(-xi) ≤ 1/8
  prefactor : uniformRate ≤ xi*Real.log s/16
  logtwo : Real.log 2 ≤ xi*Real.log s/8

structure ScaleReady (r : ℕ) : Prop where
  two : 2 ≤ r
  logone : 1 ≤ (r:ℝ)*Real.log 2
  absorb : 3*Real.log 2+2*(K0+1+Real.log 4) ≤ theta*((r:ℝ)*Real.log 2)/8
  size : ∀ s : ℝ, (2:ℝ)^r ≤ s → SizeReady s

def slack (s : ℝ) : ℝ :=
  if 1 < s then s ^ (-((2 : ℕ) : ℝ) * rho * s) * (s ^ rho - 1) else 1

end SparseScalar

def SparsePerturb (r : ℕ) (zStar : ℝ) : Prop :=
  0 < zStar ∧ zStar ≤ baseZeta r ∧ ∀ G : Grid, G.b ≤ SparseScalar.B0 → G.Allowed r →
    ∀ z ∈ Set.Icc 0 zStar, ∀ E : Finset G.Choices, E.Nonempty →
      G.eventDistance E z < SparseScalar.slack G.size

/-- The quantitative all-grid estimate under the explicit scale and perturbation bounds. -/
theorem all_grid_moment {r : ℕ} (hr : ScaleLarge r) (hrS : SparseScalar.ScaleReady r)
    (hS : ∀ G : Grid, G.Allowed r → ScalarInduction.MainSizeReady SparseScalar.q G.size)
    {zStar : ℝ} (hζ : SparsePerturb r zStar) {z : ℝ} (hz : z∈Set.Icc 0 zStar) :
    ∀ G : Grid, G.Allowed r → MomentBound G SparseScalar.q z := by
  sorry

/-- One global choice of scale, moment order, and positive perturbation interval. -/
theorem conditional_main : ConditionalMain := by
  sorry

end CoordinateSweeps

end

end OAI
```

```json
      "statement_lean": "abbrev Choices (G : Grid) := ∀ j, G.Line j → Equiv.Perm (Cube (G.bits j))"
    },
    {
      "docstring": null,
      "kind": "def",
      "name": "fiberPerm",
      "statement_lean": "def fiberPerm {A B : Type*} (p : B → Equiv.Perm A) : Equiv.Perm (A × B) where\n  toFun x := (p x.2 x.1, x.2)\n  invFun x := ((p x.2).symm x.1, x.2)\n  left_inv x := by simp\n  right_inv x := by simp"
    },
    {
      "docstring": null,
      "kind": "def",
      "name": "stage",
      "statement_lean": "def stage (G : Grid) (ω : G.Choices) (j : Fin G.b) : Equiv.Perm G.Slot :=\n  (Equiv.piSplitAt j (fun k => Cube (G.bits k))).trans\n    ((fiberPerm (ω j)).trans (Equiv.piSplitAt j (fun k => Cube (G.bits k))).symm)"
    },
    {
      "docstring": null,
      "kind": "def",
      "name": "boundary",
      "statement_lean": "def boundary (G : Grid) (ω : G.Choices) (t : ℕ) : Equiv.Perm G.Slot :=\n  ((List.ofFn (G.stage ω)).take t).reverse.prod"
    },
    {
      "docstring": null,
      "kind": "def",
      "name": "sweep",
      "statement_lean": "def sweep (G : Grid) (ω : G.Choices) : Equiv.Perm G.Slot := G.boundary ω G.b"
    },
    {
      "docstring": "The side lengths are in `[R, R²]`, where the single global scale is `R = 2^r`.",
      "kind": "def",
      "name": "Allowed",
      "statement_lean": "def Allowed (G : Grid) (r : ℕ) : Prop := ∀ j, r ≤ G.bits j ∧ G.bits j ≤ 2 * r"
    },
    {
      "docstring": "Full disjoint paths, including every boundary of the ordered sweep.",
      "kind": "structure",
      "name": "Holes",
      "statement_lean": "structure Holes (G : Grid) (h : ℕ) where\n  path : Fin h → Fin (G.b + 1) → G.Slot\n  disjoint : ∀ t, Function.Injective (fun i => path i t)\n  coordinate_step : ∀ i (j : Fin G.b) (k : Fin G.b), k ≠ j →\n    path i j.succ k = path i j.castSucc k"
    },
    {
      "docstring": null,
      "kind": "def",
      "name": "Compatible",
      "statement_lean": "def Compatible (H : G.Holes h) (ω : G.Choices) : Prop :=\n  ∀ i (t : Fin (G.b + 1)), G.boundary ω t (H.path i 0) = H.path i t"
    },
    {
      "docstring": null,
      "kind": "def",
      "name": "Feasible",
      "statement_lean": "def Feasible (H : G.Holes h) : Prop := ∃ ω, H.Compatible ω"
    },
    {
      "docstring": null,
      "kind": "def",
      "name": "lineCount",
      "statement_lean": "def lineCount (H : G.Holes h) (j : Fin G.b) (L : G.Line j) : ℕ :=\n  (Finset.univ.filter (fun i : Fin h =>\n    (fun k : {k : Fin G.b // k ≠ j} => H.path i j.castSucc k) = L)).card"
    },
    {
      "docstring": null,
      "kind": "def",
      "name": "cost",
      "statement_lean": "def cost (H : G.Holes h) : ℝ :=\n  ∑ j, ∑ L : G.Line j,\n    Real.log (((2 ^ G.bits j : ℕ) : ℝ) ^ H.lineCount j L /\n      ((2 ^ G.bits j).descFactorial (H.lineCount j L) : ℝ))"
    },
    {
      "docstring": "The symmetric group on the unmarked input sites.",
      "kind": "def",
      "name": "stabilizer",
      "statement_lean": "def stabilizer (H : G.Holes h) : Subgroup (Equiv.Perm G.Slot) where\n  carrier g := ∀ i, g (H.path i 0) = H.path i 0\n  one_mem' _ := rfl\n  mul_mem' := by\n    intro g g' hg hg' i\n    change g (g' (H.path i 0)) = H.path i 0\n    rw [hg' i, hg i]\n  inv_mem' hg i := (Equiv.symm_apply_eq _).mpr (hg i).symm"
    },
    {
      "docstring": null,
      "kind": "def",
      "name": "reference",
      "statement_lean": "def reference (H : G.Holes h) (hf : H.Feasible) : G.Choices := hf.choose"
    },
    {
      "docstring": null,
      "kind": "theorem",
      "name": "reference_compatible",
      "statement_lean": "theorem reference_compatible (H : G.Holes h) (hf : H.Feasible) :\n    H.Compatible (H.reference hf) := hf.choose_spec"
    },
    {
      "docstring": "A reference completion identifies the free input and output sites.",
      "kind": "def",
      "name": "residual",
      "statement_lean": "def residual (H : G.Holes h) (hf : H.Feasible) (ω : {ω : G.Choices // H.Compatible ω}) :\n    H.stabilizer :=\n  ⟨(G.sweep (H.reference hf))⁻¹ * G.sweep ω.val, by\n    intro i\n    change (G.sweep (H.reference hf)).symm (G.sweep ω.val (H.path i 0)) = H.path i 0\n    apply (Equiv.symm_apply_eq _).mpr\n    exact (ω.property i (Fin.last _)).trans ((H.reference_compatible hf i (Fin.last _)).symm)⟩"
    },
    {
      "docstring": "Independent line permutations with law `(1-z) U + z B`.",
      "kind": "def",
      "name": "choiceWeight",
      "statement_lean": "def choiceWeight (G : Grid) (z : ℝ) (ω : G.Choices) : ℝ :=\n  ∏ j, ∏ L : G.Line j,\n    ((1-z) * (Fintype.card (Equiv.Perm (Cube (G.bits j))) : ℝ)⁻¹ +\n      z * binaryLaw (G.bits j) (ω j L))"
    },
    {
      "docstring": null,
      "kind": "def",
      "name": "probability",
      "statement_lean": "def probability (H : G.Holes h) (z : ℝ) : ℝ :=\n  ∑ ω : {ω : G.Choices // H.Compatible ω}, G.choiceWeight z ω.val"
    },
    {
      "docstring": null,
      "kind": "def",
      "name": "eventDistance",
      "statement_lean": "def eventDistance (G : Grid) (E : Finset G.Choices) (z : ℝ) : ℝ :=\n  ∑ ω ∈ E, |G.choiceWeight z ω/(∑ v ∈ E, G.choiceWeight z v)-\n    G.choiceWeight 0 ω/(∑ v ∈ E, G.choiceWeight 0 v)|"
    },
    {
      "docstring": null,
      "kind": "structure",
      "name": "UnitaryIrrep",
      "statement_lean": "structure UnitaryIrrep (Γ : Type*) [Group Γ] where\n  dimension : ℕ\n  positive : 0 < dimension\n  matrix : Γ →* Matrix (Fin dimension) (Fin dimension) ℂ\n  unitary : ∀ g, (matrix g).conjTranspose * matrix g = 1\n  irreducible : ∀ S : Submodule ℂ (Fin dimension → ℂ),\n    (∀ g v, v ∈ S → (matrix g).mulVec v ∈ S) → S = ⊥ ∨ S = ⊤"
    },

      "kind": "def",
      "name": "schattenMoment",
      "statement_lean": "def schattenMoment {D : ℕ} (q : ℕ) (K : Matrix (Fin D) (Fin D) ℂ) : ℝ :=\n  (Matrix.trace ((K.conjTranspose * K) ^ q)).re"
    },
    {
      "docstring": null,
      "kind": "def",
      "name": "conditionalAverage",
      "statement_lean": "def conditionalAverage (H : G.Holes h) (hf : H.Feasible) (z : ℝ)\n    (ρ : UnitaryIrrep H.stabilizer) : Matrix (Fin ρ.dimension) (Fin ρ.dimension) ℂ :=\n  (H.probability z : ℂ)⁻¹ •\n    ∑ ω : {ω : G.Choices // H.Compatible ω},\n      (G.choiceWeight z ω.val : ℂ) • ρ.matrix (H.residual hf ω)"
    },

    "text": "The following describes the scope of the Lean formalization related to the following accompanying paper(s):\n\n- [Optimal-order mixing of the Thorp shuffle](../../preprints/Optimal-order-mixing-of-the-Thorp-shuffle-September-26-2026/paper.pdf)\n- [From partial permutation information to Fourier bounds](../../preprints/From-partial-permutation-information-to-Fourier-bounds-September-26-2026/main.pdf)\n- [Conditional permutations in a revealed switching environment](../../preprints/Conditional-permutations-in-a-revealed-switching-environment-September-26-2026/paper.pdf)\n- [Routing densities and representation contraction for Thorp sweeps](../../preprints/Routing-densities-and-representation-contraction-for-Thorp-sweeps-September-26-2026/paper.pdf)\n- [Row–column symmetry and contraction of coordinate sweeps](../../preprints/Row-column-symmetry-and-contraction-of-coordinate-sweeps-September-26-2026/paper.pdf)\n- [Random-subspace tests and trace smoothing for coordinate sweeps](../../preprints/Random-subspace-tests-and-trace-smoothing-for-coordinate-sweeps-September-26-2026/paper.pdf)\n- [Compatibility entropy and the spectrum of a Thorp sweep](../../preprints/Compatibility-entropy-and-the-spectrum-of-a-Thorp-sweep-September-26-2026/paper.pdf)\n- [Signed tensor densities and diagram budgets for the Thorp shuffle](../../preprints/Signed-tensor-densities-and-diagram-budgets-for-coordinate-sweeps-September-26-2026/paper.pdf)\n- [Conditional coordinate sweeps and analytic transfer](../../preprints/Conditional-coordinate-sweeps-and-analytic-transfer-September-26-2026/main.pdf)\n- [A strict four-row permanent inequality and permutation moments](../../preprints/A-strict-four-row-permanent-inequality-and-permutation-moments-September-26-2026/main.pdf)\n\n## Scope\n\nThe formalization proves optimal-order mixing of the Thorp shuffle on $2^d$ cards. After $1600d$ complete shuffles, the full permutation law converges in total variation to uniform as $d\\to\\infty$, uniformly over initial decks. Together with the support lower bound of $2d-O(1)$, this gives mixing time $\\Theta(d)=\\Theta(\\log(2^d))$.\n\nThe linked supporting results include frame and conditional-list estimates, regular trace and spectral bounds, signed moments, and full-density $L^2$ control. They also bound reciprocal Specht-dimension sums and give the eight-block Fourier estimate used in the mixing argument.\n\nThe formalized supporting result turns bounds on partial-permutation cosets into Fourier bounds. Partition a finite set into $b\\ge1$ nonempty blocks, and let $f\\ge0$ be a subprobability weight on its symmetric group whose left-coset masses for each block subgroup are at most $B$. For every irreducible unitary representation of dimension $D$ and every $u>0$, both the squared Hilbert–Schmidt norm and squared operator norm of its Fourier transform are at most $bB\\,C(u)D^{-1+(u+2)/b}$, where $C(u)$ is the stated symmetric-group degree constant.\n\nThe paper's asymptotic conclusion about the product of two random permutations is outside this selected finite estimate.\n\nThe formalized results give averaged conditional mixing for half-permutations in the revealed-path model and full-deck mixing for the Thorp shuffle. In particular, the full-deck total-variation distance tends to zero after $16040400d$ steps on $2^d$ cards, uniformly over deterministic initial decks. The conditional estimate is averaged over the actual outside-path law, rather than asserted for each individual environment. The formalization also includes the associated overlay, reset minorization, and two-color conditional constructions.\n\nThe formalization covers nine routing and representation estimates for Thorp sweeps: adaptive operator and fourth-trace bounds, Casimir moments, dense truncation, sparse contact, harmonic-cycle contraction, smoothing, and three tail or sparse-saving estimates. They bound routing-density deviations and Fourier operators in dense and sparse regimes, including exponential savings in the level scale and representation dimension.\n\nThe conditional high-height estimate requires a sufficiently large cutoff height $J$ that exceeds twice the number $s$ of coordinates in the fixed low block, so $J>2s$. These are the earlier nine statements associated with the paper; its changed later statements and six other result blocks are outside this scope.\n\nThe formalization proves uniform representation bounds for one coordinate sweep of the Thorp shuffle. For each irreducible representation of dimension $D$, it chooses a weight between $D^{3/4}$ and $D$ and a Schatten exponent bounded by one absolute constant so that the weighted Schatten moment is at most one. The sweep's operator norm is consequently at most $D^{-c}$ for one absolute $c>0$.\n\nThe supporting row–column estimate bounds the total squared overlap over all multiplicity copies on an occupied board, with explicit dependence on the row and column representation dimensions, the global dimension, the number of missing cells, and the number of rows or columns containing the global Young diagram. The paper's full physical-mixing conclusion is outside these selected representation estimates.\n\nThe formalization proves a uniform trace-smoothing estimate for coordinate sweeps of the Thorp shuffle on $2^d$ cards. One absolute sweep parameter works for every $d\\ge1$: the regular trace is at most $1+(2^d)^{-10}$, and the full permutation law after the corresponding fixed number of sweeps is within $\\tfrac12(2^d)^{-5}$ of uniform in total variation, for every initial deck. Thus the selected upper bound uses $O(d)$ physical shuffles.\n\nThe formalized result is the weighted compatibility theorem for balanced $A\\times D$ rectangles, with $n=AD$ and $\\sqrt n/2\\le A,D\\le2\\sqrt n$. For arbitrary nonnegative weights on the row and column permutation groups, the normalized compatibility average is bounded by $\\exp(C_0n^{54/100})$ times the product of the marginal $L^{1/\\theta}$ factors, where $\\theta=1-L/\\log\\sqrt n>0$ and the constants are absolute. Compatibility means injectivity in every original column. The bounded regular-moment and other moment/rank conclusions are not included.\n\nThe formalization gives signed representation estimates for coordinate sweeps of the Thorp shuffle. For every signed occurrence of a Young diagram of size $2^d$, it bounds the logarithm of a fixed-order weighted sweep moment by a small multiple of the signed partition entropy plus an explicit remainder-size budget. The moment order is uniform over the diagrams and decompositions after the two small coefficients are fixed.\n\nThe linked supporting angle bound controls the overlap of row, column, and global signed-type projections on an occupied rectangle, with explicit entropy, dimension, and missing-cell factors. These selected estimates support the paper's full-density argument; the full $L^2$ and total-variation mixing conclusion is outside them.\n\nThe formalized binary-sweep theorem gives a universal power contraction in every irreducible representation: for sufficiently large $d$, the averaged sweep on $2^d$ slots has operator norm at most $D^{-g}$ in representation dimension $D$, for some $g>0$. The sign expectation is zero, and a fixed number of independent sweeps approaches uniform in total variation from every initial deck. A separate conditional moment estimate covers allowed power-of-two grids and disjoint coordinate-respecting trajectories, with its stated feasibility premise. Physical-shuffle law identification is not included.\n\nThe formalization proves the paper's strict four-row permanent inequality. There are constants $4/3<p<2$ and $\\varepsilon>0$ such that every probability law on $S_4$ within total-variation distance $\\varepsilon$ of uniform, and with exactly uniform coordinate marginals, satisfies the permanent bound by the product of the four $L^p$ row norms for every nonnegative matrix. The exponent and neighborhood are uniform over those laws. The Thorp mixing consequence is outside this selected statement."
  },
```

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


Fix half the labels in a Thorp shuffle on $`2^d`$ positions and reveal their complete trajectories. We prove that, after an explicit absolute number of coordinate sweeps, the conditional permutation of the remaining labels approaches uniform in expected total variation as $`d\to\infty`$, uniformly in the initial layout. The resulting constructions give full-deck mixing in a constant multiple of <i>d</i> physical shuffles.
